e_{23}\in E(C)
, the set of edges from
E_{23}\setminus E(C)
is now exactly equal to
E_{out}
. Due to the choice of
e
, it follows that
the number of edges crossing any
e_{23}
does not increase after the transformation. However, due to
k
-minimality of
\mathcal{I}
, it follows that the number of crossings before and after the transformation must remain the same.
After iteratively transforming
\sigma^{\prime}
in this manner for each
C\in{\sf nice}
, in the final drawing
\sigma^{\prime}
, we ensure the following properties for each
C\in{\sf nice}
:

1.

C
is consecutive in
\sigma^{\prime}
. Further, if
C
was
\sigma
-left (resp.
\sigma
-right), then
C
is
\sigma^{\prime}
-left (resp.
\sigma
-right).

2.

All the vertices in
V(C)\cap V_{2}
belong to the interval
\left\{p\in V_{2}:\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(p^{l}(C))<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(p)<\sigma^{\prime}2(p^{r}(C))\right\}
defined by the vertices
p^{l}(C),p^{r}(C)
,

3.

All the vertices in
V(C)\cap V_{3}
belong to the interval
\left\{q\in V_{2}:\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(q^{l}(C))<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(q)<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(q^{r}(C))\right\}
defined by the vertices
q^{l}(C),q^{r}(C)
, and

4.

For any distinct
C,C^{\prime}\in{\sf nice}
, the edges of
E(C)
and
E(C^{\prime})
do not cross each other.

These properties imply that, the final drawing
\sigma^{\prime}
obtained at the end of step 1 is
({\sf Sep},\sigma)
-compatible, where
\sigma
is the original drawing from the beginning of the proof. We refer to the pair of intervals defined in items 2 and 3 above as the
sub-slot
defined by
p^{l}(C),p^{r}(C),q^{l}(C),q^{r}(C)
, and denote it by
\mathsf{subslot}(C)
. We note that, for any
C\in{\sf nice}
, since
E(C)\cup\left\{uv:u\in C\right\}
does not contain any edge of
{\sf CrossingEdges}\cup{\sf MiddleEdges}
, it holds that
either (i) for each
u\in{\sf lsep}_{23}(C)\cap V_{i}
,
\sigma^{\prime}_{i}(u)<\sigma^{\prime}_{i}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},i))
, for
i\in\left\{2,3\right\}
or (ii) for each
u\in{\sf lsep}_{23}(C)\cap V_{i}
,
\sigma^{\prime}_{i}(u)>\sigma^{\prime}_{i}({\sf endpt}({\sf rsep}_{23},i))
, for
i\in\left\{2,3\right\}
. Informally, either the entire subslot—and hence—is to the left of
{\sf lsep}_{12}
, or to the right of
{\sf rsep}_{23}
.

Further, let
E_{out}(C)\subseteq E_{23}\setminus E(C)
to be the set of all edges that cross all of the edges of
E_{23}
in
\sigma^{\prime}
.

Step 2. Constructing an Extended Component Blueprint.

Recall that each reduced type
(i,j)\in\mathsf{Types}\setminus\left\{(L,\ast),(\ast,L)\right\}
is the same as the corresponding original type; whereas the two special types
(L,\ast),(\ast,L)
consist of multiple original types. We first extend the notion of a component-blueprint to original types, instead of reduced types. Note that an important difference here is that, this definition does not the “exceptional set”
\mathcal{E}
.

Definition 36
.

An
extended component-blueprint
is a tuple

{\sf ExtComponentBlueprint}=({\sf LeftNum},{\sf RightNum},{\sf optL},{\sf optR}),

such that,

•

{\sf LeftNum},{\sf RightNum}:\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
, where for each original type
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, it holds that
{\sf LeftNum}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})+{\sf RightNum}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})=|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})|
.

•

{\sf optL},{\sf optR}:\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
, where for each original type
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, it holds that
{\sf optL}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})+{\sf optR}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})={\sf opt}(\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}))
.

We similarly define the notion of an extended component-guess, and the compliance thereof with an extended component-blueprint.

Next, we will look at the current
\sigma^{\prime}
to define an
{\sf ExtComponentBlueprint}^{o}=({\sf LeftNum}^{o},{\sf RightNum}^{o},{\sf optL}^{o},{\sf optR}^{o})
that will be used as a reference point. To this end, define for each original type
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
,

•

({\sf LeftComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}),{\sf RightComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}))
denote the partition of
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, where
{\sf LeftComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is the set of components that are
\sigma^{\prime}
-left, and
{\sf RightComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is the set of components that are
\sigma^{\prime}
-right.

•

{\sf LeftNum}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq|{\sf LeftComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})|
, and
{\sf RightNum}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq|{\sf RightComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})|
.

•

{\sf optL}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq{\sf opt}({\sf LeftComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}))
, and
{\sf optR}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq{\sf opt}({\sf RightComps}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}))
.

Now, consider any
{\sf ExtComponentGuess}=({\sf LeftComps},{\sf RightComps})
that is
{\sf ExtComponentBlueprint}^{o}
-compliant. In this step, we will iteratively modify
\sigma^{\prime}
such that, for each
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, all the components in
{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
are
\sigma^{\prime}
-left, and are placed consecutively, and similarly, all the components in
{\sf RightComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
are
\sigma^{\prime}
-right, and are placed consecutively.

We iterate over all original types
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
in an arbitrary order. Fix one such
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, and let
C^{l}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq\arg\min_{C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}|{\sf SlotEdges}(C)|\cdot i^{\prime}+|E_{out}(C)|\cdot j^{\prime}
, where
{\sf SlotEdges}(C)\subseteq E_{12}
denotes the set of edges that cross any edge of the form
vu
, where
u
belongs to the same as
C
. Let
\mathsf{subslot}^{l}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq\mathsf{subslot}(C(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}))
. We transform
\sigma^{\prime}
as follows: for each
C^{\prime}\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, we move all the vertices of
C^{\prime}
to the
\mathsf{subslot}^{l}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
while maintaining the same relative order of vertices of
C^{\prime}
. We place all such components one after the other in an arbitrary order in this subslot. We perform a similar transformation for
{\sf RightComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
. Note that after completely processing each original type
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, the resultant drawing
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
at the end of the iteration, is
({\sf Sep},\sigma^{\prime})
-compatible, where
\sigma^{\prime}
is the drawing before the start of the iteration.

Note that for
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\left\{1,2,\ldots,(k)^{1/3}\right\}^{2}
, the original and reduced types are the same. Hence, this step makes all such
{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime}),{\sf RightComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
consecutive. For each
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, let
c^{l}_{12}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq|{\sf SlotEdges}(C^{l}(i,j))|,c^{l}_{23}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\coloneqq|E_{out}(C^{l}(i,j)|
, where
C^{l}(i,j)
is the component that is used to relocate all other components. Analogously, define
c^{r}_{12}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
and
c^{r}_{23}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
.

Step 3. Handling reduced type
(\ast,L)
,
(L,\ast)
.

Let
T_{orig}(L,\ast)
be the set of all original types whose corresponding reduced type is
(L,\ast)
. Note that these consist of original types
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, where
i<k^{1/3}
and
j\geq(k)^{1/3}
. Also define
T_{orig}(\ast,L)
as the set of original types
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
with
i\geq(k)^{1/3}
.

We first consider the left side. We classify the original types
T_{orig}(L,\ast)
into two sub-categories
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in T_{orig}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is
left-category 0
if
c^{l}_{12}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})=0
; and
left-category 1
if
c^{l}_{12}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\geq 1
. Similarly, classify the original types
T_{orig}(\ast,L)
into two sub-categories as follows:
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in T_{orig}(\ast,L)
is
left-category 0
if
c^{l}_{23}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})=0
; and
left-category 1
if
c^{l}_{23}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\geq 1
. Similarly, define the notion of right-category 0 and right-category 1, respectively.

First, we show how to handle left-category 0 types from
(L,\ast)
. Let these original types be
(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),\ldots,(i_{q},j_{q})
. By definition, for each
1\leq p\leq q
,
c^{l}_{12}(i_{p},j_{p})=0
. Let
\ell=\argmin_{1\leq p\leq q}c^{l}_{23}(i_{p},j_{p})
. We move all components in
{\sf LeftComps}(i_{p},j_{p})
to the subslot containing all the components of
{\sf LeftComps}(i_{\ell},j_{\ell})
, while preserving the relative order within the components of
{\sf LeftComps}(i_{p},j_{p})
for each
1\leq p\leq q
, as well as relative order of vertices within each component. Note that, for each component
C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i_{p},j_{p})
, the number of crossings from outside
E(C)
, before the movement is
c_{23}(i_{p},j_{p})
, and that after the movement is
c_{23}(i_{\ell},j_{\ell})\leq c_{23}(i_{p},j_{p})
. Therefore, we do not increase the number of crossings in this transformation.

We handle left-category 0 types from
(\ast,L)
, along with reduced type
(1,0)
in a very similar manner, which we describe for completeness. Let
(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),\ldots,(i_{q},j_{q})
, indicate the original types corresponding to
(\ast,L)
such that
1\leq p\leq q
,
c^{l}_{23}(i_{p},j_{p})=0
. Let
\ell=\argmin_{1\leq p\leq q}c^{l}_{12}(i_{p},j_{p})
. We move all components in
{\sf LeftComps}(i_{p},j_{p})
to the subslot containing all the components of
{\sf LeftComps}(i_{\ell},j_{\ell})
, while preserving the relative order within the components of
{\sf LeftComps}(i_{p},j_{p})
for each
1\leq p\leq q
, as well as relative order of vertices within each component. Note that, for each component
C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i_{p},j_{p})
, the number of crossings of
{\sf ConnEdges}
from outside, before the movement is
c^{l}_{12}(i_{p},j_{p})
, and that after the movement is
c^{l}_{12}(i_{\ell},j_{\ell})\leq c^{l}_{12}(i_{p},j_{p})
. Therefore, we do not increase the number of crossings in this transformation.

We do a similar transformation for right-category 0 types from
T_{orig}(L,\ast)
and
T_{orig}(\ast,L)
to move the components
{\sf RightComps}(\cdot,\cdot)
of the respective types. We omit the description.

Finally, we handle left/right-category 1 types from
(\ast,L)
and
(L,\ast)
by showing that the total number of components belonging to such types is bounded by
(k)^{2/3}
. Formally, we show the following claim.

Claim 5.6
.

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(i,j):\max(i,j)\geq(k)^{1/3}\\
(i,j)\text{ is left/right-category 1}\end{subarray}}|\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(i,j)|\leq(k)^{2/3}.

Proof of
5.6
.

We prove that each component
C
of original type
(i,j)
with
(i,j)
being left/right-category 1, accounts for a distinct set of
(k)^{1/3}
crossings. Since the total number of crossings is
k
, this will show the claim.

First consider
C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
with
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in T_{orig}(L,\ast)
, such that
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is left-category 1 (the proof for right-category 1 is analogous). Since
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is left-category 1,
c^{l}_{12}(i,j)\geq 1
. Note that
|{\sf ConnEdges}|\geq k^{1/3}
. By the previous step, all the components in
{\sf LeftComps}^{\prime}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
belong to a single slot, and by
5.2
, all the edges in
{\sf ConnEdges}
cross the same non-empty set of edges – where the non-emptiness follows from
c^{l}_{12}(i,j)\geq 1
. Thus, there are
i\geq(k)^{1/3}
such edges, and each edge is involved in at least
(k)^{1/3}
crossings.

Now let us consider
C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, where
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in T_{orig}(\ast,L)
and
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is left-category 1 (the proof for right-category 1 is analogous). Since
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is category 1,
c^{l}_{23}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\geq 1
. Further, all the components in
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
belong to a single slot. Further, by step 1, the edges of different components
C,C^{\prime}\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
do not cross each other; and all the edges of
\bigcup_{C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}E(C)
cross the same non-empty set of edges not in
\bigcup_{C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})}E(C)
– here, the non-emptiness follows from
c^{l}_{23}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\geq 1
. Thus, for each
C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, each edge in
E(C)
is crossed at least once, and there are at least
j\geq(k)^{1/3}
edges in
E(C)
, which amounts to at least
(k)^{1/3}
crossings for each
C\in{\sf LeftComps}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{E}
denote the set of all components
C\in\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
, where
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
is left/right-category 1.
5.6
shows that
|\mathcal{E}|\leq k^{2/3}
. Now, we use this
\mathcal{E}
to obtain a component-blueprint and component-guess.

Recall the extended component-blueprint
{\sf ExtComponentBlueprint}^{o}=({\sf LeftNum}^{o},{\sf RightNum}^{o},{\sf optL}^{o},{\sf optR}^{o})
defined earlier. Now, define
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}^{p}=({\sf LeftNum}^{p},{\sf RightNum}^{p},{\sf optL}^{p},{\sf optR}^{p},\mathcal{E})
, where for each
(i,j)\in\mathsf{Types}\setminus\left\{(L,\ast),(\ast,L)\right\}
, define
{\sf LeftNum}^{p}(i,j)={\sf LeftNum}^{o}(i,j)
,
{\sf RightNum}^{p}(i,j)={\sf RightNum}^{o}(i,j)
,
{\sf optL}^{p}(i,j)={\sf optL}^{o}(i,j)
, and
{\sf optR}^{p}(i,j)={\sf optR}^{o}(i,j)
. On the other hand, for
(i,j)\in\left\{(L,\ast),(\ast,L)\right\}
, define

•

{\sf LeftNum}^{p}(i,j)=\sum_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})T_{orig}(i,j)\text{ and left-category 0}}{\sf LeftNum}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
,

•

{\sf RightNum}^{p}(i,j)=\sum_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in T_{orig}(i,j)\text{ and right-category 0}}{\sf RightNum}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
,

•

{\sf optL}^{p}(i,j)=\sum_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})T_{orig}(i,j)\text{ and left-category 0}}{\sf optL}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
,

•

{\sf optR}^{p}(i,j)=\sum_{(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})T_{orig}(i,j)\text{ and left-category 0}}{\sf optR}^{o}(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
.

Now, notice that, any
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}^{p}
-compliant guess
{\sf ComponentGuess}^{p}
can be extended to
{\sf ExtComponentBlueprint}^{o}
-compliant
{\sf ExtComponentGuess}^{o}
, by considering any partition of the category 1 types with appropriate constraints, which is essentially a partition of components in
\mathcal{E}
. Then, applying the 3-step proof as described above, we can show that, for the
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}^{p}
as defined above, for any
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}^{p}
-compliant
{\sf ComponentGuess}
, the drawing
\sigma^{\prime}
obtained at the end belongs to
{\sf NiceCFamily}({\sf ComponentBlueprint}^{p},{\sf ComponentGuess},{\sf Sep},\sigma)
, and also is
({\sf Sep},\sigma)
-compatible. This completes the proof.

∎

5.4
Step 2: Guessing the Interfaces

In this step, we will “guess” various subsets of edges and vertices that can be used in the creation of two sub-instances in the next step. Each of the guesses are made by supposing that
\mathcal{I}
is a
k
-minimal
Yes
-instance with an unknown drawing
\sigma
. Further, we describe the algorithm corresponding to bottom-heavy case, i.e., when
k_{12}\geq k_{23}
, which implies that
k_{12}\geq k/2
– top-heavy case is symmetric by exchanging the roles of lines 1 and 3. These guesses will be made in multiple steps, with each step being dependent on all possible choices of the previous steps. Further, if any of the guesses in a subsequent step contradicts a guess done at a previous step, then such a guess is deemed invalid and is terminated. These guesses can be thought of
branching
on each of the specified choices in all steps. If all the guesses are terminated, or the corresponding recursive calls report that the sub-instances are
No
-instances, then we will conclude that our assumption that
\mathcal{I}
was a
Yes
-instance was wrong, and thus we must have a
No
-instance.

In each step, we specify at a high level the kind of object that we are trying to guess. Whenever we are trying to guess a subset of edges, we follow the following convention: all the edges of a multi-edge are treated in the same manner, such as being chosen in a guess.

Step A: Separator.

We start by guessing the separator
{\sf Sep}=\left\{{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf rsep}_{12},{\sf lsep}_{23},{\sf rsep}_{23}\right\}
w.r.t. a hypothetical (unknown) drawing
\sigma
. Note that there are at most
m^{4}=n^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
choices. Furthermore, based on our guess, we can also conclude whether we are in (
DB
,
DT
), (
DB
,
ST
), (
SB
,
DT
), or (
SB
,
ST
) case. Next, we guess
{\sf MiddleEdges}({\sf Sep})\subseteq E_{12}
of size at most
2\sqrt{k}
, and two sets
{\sf GapLeft},{\sf GapRight}\subseteq V_{2}^{conn}
of size at most
3\sqrt{k}
, as in the definition of
Definition

17
. There are at most
n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
choices for this. Overall, we have at most
n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
choices.

Step B: Labeling
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf BoundaryEdges})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})\cup{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}
.

The vertices of
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf leftbound}_{12})
and
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf leftbound}_{23})
receive labels
{\cal L}
. The vertices of
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf lsep}_{12})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf lsep}_{23})\cup{\sf GapLeft}
are each given two labels
{\cal L},{\cal M}
. Similarly, the vertices of
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf rsep}_{12})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf rsep}_{23})\cup{\sf GapRight}
are given two labels
{\cal M},{\cal R}
. Note that the assignment of labels in this step does not overwrite previous labels. For example, in
SB
/
ST
case the shared bottom (resp. top) endpoint of
{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf lsep}_{23}
(resp.
{\sf lsep}_{23},{\sf rsep}_{23}
) gets all three labels. Similarly, if
{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf lsep}_{23}
share top endpoint, then the shared endpoint will also receive all three labels. Note that there is no guessing involved in this step.

Step C:
{\sf CrossingEdges}
and labeling their endpoints.

For each edge
e\in{\sf Sep}
, we guess
{\sf cross}(e)
, which is a set of at most
\sqrt{k}
edges that cross
e
. Let
{\sf CrossingEdges}\coloneqq\bigcup_{e\in{\sf Sep}}{\sf cross}(e)
.

We now describe feasible pairs for labels of the endpoints of
{\sf CrossingEdges}
; and each guess corresponds to using labeling the endpoint of each edge in
{\sf CrossingEdges}
by one of the feasible (ordered) pairs. However, if the guess for the label is
incompatible
with a previously assigned label, then we consider it as an invalid guess and proceed to the next one. Note that, if a vertex has previously been assigned multiple labels, then the incompatible label(s) is a label that has not been assigned to the said vertex, if any.

•

For
e\in{\sf cross}({\sf lsep}_{12})\cup{\sf cross}({\sf lsep}_{23})
, the feasible label pairs are
({\cal L},{\cal M}),({\cal M},{\cal L}),({\cal L},{\cal R}),({\cal R},{\cal L})
.

•

For
e\in{\sf cross}({\sf rsep}_{12})\cup{\sf cross}({\sf rsep}_{23})
, the feasible label pairs are
({\cal R},{\cal M}),({\cal M},{\cal R}),({\cal L},{\cal R}),({\cal R},{\cal L})
.

Overall, we have at most
\binom{m}{4\sqrt{k}}^{4}\cdot 2^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}=n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
choices. Further, let
{\sf CrossLeft,CrossMid,}

{\sf CrossRight}\subseteq{\sf Endpoints}({\sf CrossingEdges})
denote the set of vertices that receive labels
{\cal L},{\cal M},{\cal R}
, respectively.

Step D: Edges incident to
{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}
.

•

Consider an edge
uv\in E_{12}\setminus{\sf CrossingEdges}
, where
v\in{\sf GapLeft}
(resp.
v\in{\sf GapRight}
). Then, we label
u
as
{\cal L}
(resp.
{\cal R}
).

•

Consider an edge
uv\in E_{23}\setminus{\sf CrossingEdges}
, where
v\in{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}
. Then, we label
u
as
{\cal M}
.

Step E: Handling
{\sf comps}(v)
for
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
.

Recall that for each
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
, each component
C\in{\sf comps}(v)
can be classified into
{\sf Special}(v),\pi\text{\sf-Defined}(v),{\sf UpDown}(v),{\sf PeculiarNP}(v)
, and
{\sf PeculiarNP}(v)
according to
Definition

19
. Now we describe how to handle each of the components separately.

\blacktriangleright

{\sf Special}(v)
.

Some of the vertices in such components are already labeled. We will return to the remaining vertices in these components at a later step.

\blacktriangleright

\pi\text{\sf-Defined}(v)
.

Recall that each
\pi_{i}
for
i\in[3]
is comprised of
\lambda=\mathcal{O}(\log k^{\star})
total orders
\pi_{i}^{1},\pi_{i}^{2},\ldots,\pi_{i}^{\lambda}
for
V_{i}^{1},V_{i}^{2},\ldots,V_{i}^{\lambda}
, respectively. For each
i\in[3]
, and for each
j\in[\lambda]
, we do the following.

•

We guess two vertices
u(j,l),w(j,r)\in V^{j}_{i}
with
\pi^{j}_{i}(u(j,l))<\pi^{j}_{i}(w(j,r))
.

•

Any
C\in\pi\text{\sf-Defined}(v)
that contains a vertex
u\in V^{j}_{i}
with
\pi^{j}_{i}(u)\leq\pi^{j}_{i}(u(j,l))
, we label all the vertices of
C
using
{\cal L}
.

•

Any
C\in\pi\text{\sf-Defined}(v)
that contains a vertex
w\in V^{j}_{i}
with
\pi^{j}_{i}(w)\geq\pi^{j}_{i}(w(j,r))
, we label all the vertices of
C
using
{\cal R}
.

If any of the guesses made in this step is incompatible with a previous step, or with a prior guess made in this step, then we terminate this guess. Note that the number of guesses made in this step is at most
n^{\mathcal{O}(\log k^{\star})}=n^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3})}
since
k=\Omega((k^{\star})^{2/3})
.

\blacktriangleright

{\sf UpDown}(v)
.

By
Lemma

12
, the number of such components in an
Yes
-instance is bounded by
8\sqrt{k}
; otherwise we terminate this guess. Otherwise, for each such component, we guess a label from
\left\{{\cal L},{\cal R}\right\}
and use that label for all the vertices of the component. The number of guesses is
n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
.

\blacktriangleright

{\sf Pendants}(v)
for all
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
.

•

We guess a pendant-blueprint

{\sf PendantBlueprint}=\left\{({\sf LeftPNum}(v),{\sf MidPNum}(v),{\sf RightPNum}(v)):v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})\right\}

in accordance with
Definition

22
. Note that the number of guesses for pendant-blueprints is bounded by
n^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
.

•

Now, fix a
{\sf PendantBlueprint}
. For each
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
, we use a variant of 3D knapsack to find a partition (if one exists)
({\sf LeftPendants}(v),{\sf MidPendants}(v)
,
{\sf RightPendants}(v))
of
{\sf Pendants}(v)
for each
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
in polynomial time, such that the resulting
{\sf PendantGuess}
is compatible with
{\sf PendantBlueprint}
(as defined in
Definition

34
). If such a partition does not exist, then we terminate the guess.

•

Then, for each
v\in{\sf Endpoints}(v)
, we label each pendant in
{\sf LeftPendants}(v),{\sf MidPendants}(v)
, and
{\sf RightPendants}(v)
with label
{\cal L},{\cal M}
and
{\cal R}
, respectively.

Note that the number of guesses in this step is bounded by
n^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
, and otherwise the step takes polynomial time.

At this point, for all
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},2)
, we have labeled all the components in
{\sf comps}(v)
(other
{\sf Special}(v)
as mentioned above). Thus, we are left with handling
{\sf PeculiarNP}(v)
for
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},1)\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},3)
.

\blacktriangleright

{\sf PeculiarNP}(v)
for all
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
.

We consider different cases based.

(i) (
DB
,
DT
) case: Here,
v\in{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},1)\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},3)
can be labeled with
{\cal L}/{\cal R}
as appropriate in accordance with
Lemma

14
.

(ii) (
SB
,
ST
) case,
Lemma

13
, we can guess two subsets of
P_{1}\subseteq{\sf PeculiarNP}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},1))
and
P_{2}\subseteq{\sf PeculiarNP}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3))
, each of size at most
\sqrt{k}
. Then, we consider the following two possibilities (guesses).

1.

Label all components of
P_{1}
using
{\cal L}
, and all components of
{\sf PeculiarNP}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},1))\setminus P_{1}
using
{\cal R}
.

Label all components of
P_{2}
using
{\cal R}
, and label all components of
{\sf PeculiarNP}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3))\setminus P_{2}
using
{\cal L}
.

2.

Label all components of
P_{1}
using
{\cal R}
, and all components of
{\sf PeculiarNP}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},1))\setminus P_{1}
using
{\cal L}
.

Label all components of
P_{2}
using
{\cal L}
, and label all components of
{\sf PeculiarNP}({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3))\setminus P_{2}
using
{\cal R}
.

(iii) (
SB
,
DT
) case. For the rest of the description of step E, let
v\coloneqq{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},1)={\sf endpt}({\sf rsep}_{12},1)
, and
\mathcal{C}\coloneqq{\sf PeculiarNP}(v)
.

•

We guess a subset
{\sf Exceptional}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
of size at most
11\cdot k^{2/3}
as in
Lemma

16
. Note that there are at most
n^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3})}
guesses. Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\setminus{\sf Exceptional}
.

•

For each
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, we use the two layer algorithm of
Theorem

6
to find an optimal number of crossings,
{\sf opt}(C)\leq k
(if
{\sf opt}(C)>k
, then clearly we have a
No
-instance). This takes time
|V(C)|^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}=n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
(note that we skip the kernelization step), and obtain a drawing of
C
.

•

According to
Definition

19
, we classify
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
into reduced types, and use
\mathcal{T}(i,j)\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
to denote the set of components of type
(i,j)
. Note that the number of reduced types is at most
k^{2/3}+2
.

•

Now, we guess a component-blueprint
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}
according to
Definition

33
. Note that the number of guesses for a blueprint is bounded by
n^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3})}
. Fix one such
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}=({\sf LeftNum},{\sf RightNum},{\sf optL},{\sf optR},\mathcal{E})
.

•

We try to construct a
{\sf ComponentBlueprint}
-compliant
{\sf ComponentGuess}=({\sf LeftComps},{\sf RightComps})
as follows: for each reduced type
(i,j)\in\mathsf{Types}
, we try to find a partition of
\mathcal{T}(i,j)\setminus\mathcal{E}
into

({\sf LeftComps}(i,j),{\sf RightComps}(i,j))
using a version of 4D knapsack (this runs in polynomial time) that satisfies the following two conditions:

–

|{\sf LeftComps}(i,j)|={\sf LeftNum}(i,j)
, and
|{\sf RightComps}(i,j)|={\sf RightNum}(i,j)

–

{\sf opt}({\sf LeftComps}(i,j))={\sf optL}(i,j)
, and
{\sf opt}({\sf RightComps}(i,j))={\sf optR}(i,j)
.

If such
{\sf ComponentGuess}
does not exist, then we terminate the current guess.

•

Otherwise, for each component
C\in\bigcup_{(i,j)\in\mathsf{Types}}{\sf LeftComps}(i,j)
, we label it using
{\cal L}
, and for each component
C\in\bigcup_{(i,j)\in\mathsf{Types}}{\sf RightComps}(i,j)
, we label it using
{\cal R}
.

(iv) (
DB
,
ST
) case: this case is handled symmetrically as (
SB
,
DT
) case as explained above in (iii). We omit the description.

Step F: Label propagation.

At this point, some of the vertices of the graph may still be unlabeled.

Let
A
denote the set of vertices that have already received a label, and let
A^{\cal L},A^{\cal M},A^{\cal R}\subseteq A
denote the subsets of vertices that have received label
{\cal L},{\cal M},{\cal R}
, respectively (note that, if a vertex has multiple labels, then it belongs to multiple subsets). Further, let
O\subseteq A
denote the subset of vertices that have only received one label, and again
O^{\cal L},O^{\cal M},O^{\cal R}
denote the corresponding partition of
O
.

Next, we define three sets of vertices, as follows.

\displaystyle{\sf Origin}^{\cal L}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\sf LeftExtBnd}\cup{\sf Endpoints}(\left\{{\sf leftbound}_{12},{\sf leftbound}_{23},{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf lsep}_{23}\right\})\cup{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf CrossLeft}

\displaystyle{\sf Origin}^{\cal R}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\sf RightExtBnd}\cup{\sf Endpoints}(\left\{{\sf rsep}_{12},{\sf rsep}_{23},{\sf rightbound}_{12},{\sf rightbound}_{23}\right\})\cup{\sf GapRight}\cup{\sf CrossRight}

\displaystyle{\sf Origin}^{\cal M}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf MiddleEdges})\cup{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}\cup{\sf CrossMid}

(3)

Now, we proceed as follows.

1.

For each
u\in{\sf Origin}^{\cal L}
, and each unlabeled
w\not\in A
that is reachable from
u
in the graph
G-(O^{\cal M}\cup O^{\cal R})
, we label it using
{\cal L}
.

2.

For each
u\in{\sf Origin}^{\cal R}
, and each unlabeled
w\not\in A
that is reachable from
u
in the graph
G-(O^{\cal L}\cup O^{\cal M})
, we label it using
{\cal R}
.

3.

For each
u\in{\sf Origin}^{\cal M}
, and each unlabeled
w\not\in A
that is reachable from
u
in the graph
G-(O^{\cal L}\cup O^{\cal R})
, we label it using
{\cal M}
.

During this process, we terminate the guess, if any of the following happens.

•

A vertex
w\not\in A
receives two different labels.

•

A vertex
w\in V_{1}\setminus A
that has at least one neighbor in
V_{2}
receives label
{\cal M}
.

•

A vertex
w\in V_{2}\setminus A
that has at least one neighbor in
V_{1}
receives label
{\cal M}
.

Otherwise, let
V^{\cal T}
denote the set of vertices that have received the label
{\cal T}
, for
{\cal T}\in\left\{{\cal L},{\cal M},{\cal R}\right\}
, respectively. Note that these sets are not disjoint.

Step G: Partial Order.

For each
i\in[3]
, we will guess permutation
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
(total order) of a certain subset of vertices of
V_{i}
in the drawing
\sigma
, which will be used to extend
\pi_{i}
to a new partial order
\pi^{\prime}_{i}
. Let
S\coloneqq{\sf Endpoints}({\sf BoundaryEdges}){\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf MiddleEdges})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf CrossingEdges})\cup{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}
. From the bounds on the respective sets of vertices and edges, it is straightforward to check that
|S|=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})
. For each
i\in[3]
, let
S_{i}\coloneqq V_{i}\cap S
. We guess permutations
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
of
S_{i}
for each
i\in[3]
. Since
|S_{i}|=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})
, it follows that the total number of guesses is
|S_{1}|!\cdot|S_{2}|!\cdot|S_{3}|!=(\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})!)^{3}=2^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k}\log k)}
. Next, we define
\pi^{\lambda+2}_{2}
as an ordering over
{\sf ExtBnd}\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},2)\cup{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}
by combining parts of
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{2}
, and one of the constituent total orders of
\pi^{2}
of the set
{\sf ExtBnd}
.
Let
\pi^{\lambda+1}=(\pi^{\lambda+1}_{1},\pi^{\lambda+1}_{2},\pi^{\lambda+1}_{3})
. We say that
\pi^{\lambda+1}
is
valid
if the following conditions are satisfied:

•

For each
i\in[3]
, for any distinct
u,v\in S_{i}
with
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}(u)

\pi_{i}
defines an order between
u
and
v
, then both
\pi_{i}
and
\pi_{i}^{\lambda+1}
are order
u
and
v
consistently.
22
22

22

Note that, it is possible that
u
and
v
are incomparable in
\pi_{i}
, but since they both belong to
S_{i}
, they are comparable in
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
.

•

\pi^{\lambda+1}
satisfies the following properties (that must satisfied by the separator):

–

{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf rsep}_{12}
do not cross,
{\sf rsep}_{12},{\sf rsep}_{23}
do not cross

–

For each
e\in{\sf Sep}
, the set of edges crossing
e
is exactly equal to
{\sf cross}(e)
,

–

{\sf MiddleEdges}
is exactly the set of edges that lie between
{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf rsep}_{12}

Note that the validity of
\pi^{\lambda+1}
can be checked in polynomial time. If a guess for
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
is invalid, then we terminate it and move to the next guess. Otherwise, for each
i\in\left\{1,3\right\}
, let
\pi^{\prime}_{i}=\pi_{i}\cup\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
, and
\pi^{\prime}_{2}=\pi_{2}\cup\pi^{\lambda+1}_{2}\cup\pi^{\lambda+2}_{2}
, and note that
\pi^{\prime}_{i}
is a valid extension of
\pi_{i}
, since
\pi^{\lambda+1}
is valid. Henceforth, we assume that we are working with such a
\pi^{\prime}=(\pi^{\prime}_{1},\pi^{\prime}_{2},\pi^{\prime}_{3})
for some guess for
\pi^{\lambda+1}
.

Step H: Parameter division.

First, let
k_{\sf special}
denote the number of crossings (with multiplicity) of the following form according to the prior guess:

•

e_{1}\in{\sf cross}({\sf lsep}_{23}),e_{2}\in{\sf cross}({\sf rsep}_{23})
such that
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
cross according to their ordering in
\pi^{\lambda+1}
.

Let
k_{\sf current}
denote the number of crossings among the edges of
{\sf CrossingEdges}\cup{\sf MiddleEdges}\cup{\sf Sep}
according to
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
, except those counted in
k_{\sf special}
. If
k_{\sf current}>k
, then we terminate the current guess. Otherwise, let
k^{\prime}\coloneqq k-k_{\sf current}
. Let
k^{\prime}
denote the final value. We guess three non-negative integers
0\leq k^{\cal L},k^{{\cal M}},k^{\cal R}
such that
k^{{\cal L}},k^{{\cal R}}\leq 3k/4
, and
k^{{\cal L}}+k^{{\cal M}}+k^{{\cal R}}=k^{\prime}
. Further, we also guess
k^{{\cal L}}_{12},k^{{\cal L}}_{23},k^{{\cal R}}_{12},k^{{\cal R}}_{23}\geq 0
such that
k^{{\cal L}}=k^{{\cal L}}_{12}+k^{{\cal L}}_{23}
, and
k^{{\cal R}}=k^{{\cal R}}_{12}+k^{{\cal R}}_{23}
. Note that the number of such guesses is bounded by
k^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
.

From the discussion above, the following lemma follows.

Lemma 18
.

The total number of guesses made in steps A through H, for (i) separator
{\sf Sep}
and its associated sets of vertices and edges, (ii) labels of vertices of
V(G)
, (iii) partial orders
\pi^{\prime}i
, and (iv) division of
k
into smaller parameters, is bounded by
n^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3})}
.

In the subsequent steps, we assume that we are working with one such fixed guess for the separator
{\sf Sep}
and its associated objects, labels of vertices, partial orders
\pi^{\prime}=(\pi^{\prime}_{1},\pi^{\prime}_{2},\pi^{\prime}_{3})
, and the smaller parameters.

5.5
Step 3: Creating sub-instances.

For each set of guesses made in the previous step (as stated in
Lemma

18
), we create three sub-instances (in fact, extended instances), (i) a left sub-instance
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}
, and (ii) a right sub-instance
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
, and (iii) a middle sub-instance
\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
. Among these,
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
are instances of
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
, whereas
\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
is an instance of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
. In this step (Step 3), we describe the construction of
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
, and in the next step (Step 4) we will recursively call our algorithm to find a drawing for each of the two sub-instances; or to conclude that either or both of them are
No
-instances, in which case we terminate the guess. Then, in Step 5, we will use the drawings of
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
found by the recursive algorithm to construct the middle sub-instance
\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
, which will be solved using the algorithm of
Theorem

6
.

Let
V^{{\cal L}},V^{{\cal M}},V^{{\cal R}}
denote the set of vertices that have received labels
{\cal L},{\cal M},{\cal R}
, respectively – note that, if a vertex has received multiple labels, then it belongs to all the respective subsets. Now, we proceed to the description of
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
.

Creating Left Sub-instance

\blacktriangleright
Base graph.

Let
G^{\prime{\cal L}}=G[V^{{\cal L}}]
, with its edge-set partitioned into regular and multi-edges, denoted by
{\sf RegularEdges}^{\cal L}
and
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal L}
, respectively.

\blacktriangleright
Boundary edges.

Let
{\sf BoundaryEdges}^{{\cal L}}\coloneqq\left\{{\sf leftbound}_{12}^{\cal L},{\sf rightbound}_{12}^{\cal L},{\sf rightbound}_{12}^{\cal L},{\sf rightbound}_{23}^{\cal L}\right\},
where
{\sf leftbound}_{12}^{\cal L}\coloneqq{\sf leftbound}_{12},{\sf leftbound}_{23}\coloneqq{\sf leftbound}_{23},{\sf rightbound}_{12}^{\cal L}\coloneqq{\sf lsep}_{12},{\sf rightbound}_{23}^{\cal L}\coloneqq{\sf lsep}_{23}
.

\blacktriangleright
Extended Boundary.

Let
{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal L}\coloneqq{\sf LeftExtBnd}\cap V^{\cal L}
,
{\sf RightExtBnd}^{\cal L}\coloneqq{\sf GapLeft}\setminus{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal L}
. Then,
{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal L}={\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal L}\cup{\sf RightExtBnd}^{\cal L}
.

\blacktriangleright
Multi-edges

Next, we add a few more multi-edges to the set
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal L}
, as follows:

For each (multi-)edge
uv\in{\sf CrossingEdges}
with at least one endpoint in
V^{{\cal L}}
, we identify a multi-edge
e_{add}(uv)
to be added in each case. After the case analysis, we will discuss the multiplicity of this edge, and actually add it to the graph.

•

uv\in E_{12}
, where
u\in V_{1}
has a label
{\cal L}
and
v\in V_{2}
has label
{\cal M}
or
{\cal R}
.

Note that, since
uv\in{\sf CrossingEdges}
, the vertex
v
cannot belong to
{\sf GapLeft}
and hence cannot have a label
{\cal L}
– this is ensured while guessing the labels.

Operation.

e_{add}(uv)\coloneqq(u,{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},2))
.

•

vu\in E_{12}
, where
u\in V_{2}
has label
{\cal L}
and
v\in V_{1}
has label
{\cal M}
or
{\cal R}
.

Operation.

e_{add}(uv)\coloneqq(u,{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},1))
to
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal L}
.

•

vu\in E_{23}
, where
u\in V_{3}
has label
{\cal L}
and
v\in V_{2}
has label
{\cal M}
or
{\cal R}
.

Note that the vertex
v
may belong to
{\sf GapLeft}\subseteq V^{{\cal L}}
, and
u
cannot be equal to
{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3)
.

Operation.
If
v\in{\sf GapLeft}\cup\left\{{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},2)\right\}
, then we also delete the edge
vu
from
E(G^{{\cal L}})
.

e_{add}\coloneqq({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},2),u)
.

•

uv\in E_{23}
, where
u\in V_{2}
has label
{\cal L}
and
v\in V_{3}
has label
{\cal M}
or
{\cal R}
.

Note that, since
uv\in{\sf CrossingEdges}
, the vertex
u
cannot belong to
{\sf GapLeft}
, and hence cannot have a label
{\cal L}
– this is ensured while guessing the labels.

Operation.

e_{add}(uv)\coloneqq(u,{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3))
.

Now, we proceed as follows:

•

If an edge is defined as
e_{add}
of multiple edges, say
e_{1},e_{2},\ldots
, then its multiplicity is defined as the sum of the respective multiplicities of
e_{1},e_{2},\ldots
– here we adopt the convention that, if an edge
e_{i}
belongs to
{\sf RegularEdges}
, then its multiplicity is
1
.

•

Finally, we add
e_{add}
with the combined multiplicity to
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal L}
. Here, if
e_{add}
is already in
{\sf RegularEdges}
, we do not remove it.

Let
G^{\cal L}
denote the resulting graph after adding the edges in this manner.

\blacktriangleright
Vertex set.

For each
i\in[3]
, let
V^{\cal L}_{i}\coloneqq V^{\cal L}\cap V_{i}
, and let
\pi^{\cal L}_{i}
denote
\pi^{\prime}_{i}
projected to the vertices of
V^{\cal L}
.

\blacktriangleright
Partial order

Let
\pi^{\cal L}=(\pi^{\cal L}_{1},\pi^{\cal L}_{2},\pi^{\cal L}_{3})
. Observe that
(\pi^{\cal L})^{\lambda+2}_{2}
contains an ordering of
{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal L}
that satisfies the required properties.

\blacktriangleright
Final instance.

Let
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}=(G^{\cal L},V^{\cal L}_{1},V^{\cal L}_{2},V^{\cal L}_{3},{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal L},\pi^{\cal L},k^{\cal L}_{1},k^{\cal L}_{2},k^{\cal L})
.

At this point, we check whether in the graph
G^{\cal L}
, each vertex of
V(G^{\cal L})
is reachable from some vertex of
{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal L}\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf BoundaryEdges})
in
G^{\cal L}
. If not, we terminate the guess. Otherwise, we continue to the next step.

At this point, we note the following observations akin to the two-layer algorithm.

•

As required in
Footnote

12
, the total number of partial orders in each
\pi_{i}
remains bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\log k^{\star})
, since parameter reduces by at least a constant factor in each step.

•

Note that
|{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal L}|\leq|{\sf LeftExtBnd}|\leq\alpha\sqrt{k^{\star}}
, and
|{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal R}|\leq|{\sf GapLeft}|\leq 3\sqrt{k}
, and analogous bounds hold for
|{\sf RightExtBnd}^{\cal R}|,|{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal R}|
, respectively. Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
satisfy
Item

3
.

•

Using a similar argument as in two layer case, it follows that the total sum of multiplicities of
WeightedEdges
incident to each vertex in
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})
remains bounded by
2\sqrt{k^{\star}}
, satisfying
Item

6

Creating Right Sub-instance

The right sub-instance
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}=(G^{\cal R},V^{\cal R}_{1},V^{\cal R}_{2},V^{\cal R}_{3},{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal R},\pi^{\cal R},k^{\cal R}_{1},k^{\cal R}_{2},k^{\cal R})
is created analogously, and we omit the description. Again, we check whether there exists some vertex of
V(G^{\cal R})
that is not reachable from some vertex of
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf BoundaryEdges}^{\cal R})\cup{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal R}
in
G^{\cal R}
, and terminate the guess. Otherwise, we proceed to the next step.

From the preceding constructions of
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
—assuming that the guesses are not terminated—it is straightforward to verify that they satisfy the properties of extended instances from
Definition

14
. Thus, we have the following claim, whose proof is omitted.

Claim 5.7
.

\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
are valid extended instances of
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
.

5.6
Step 4: Conquer left and right.

For each guess made in Step 2, we have defined a pair of sub-instances
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
in Step 3, with each having parameter at most
3k/4
. For each such pair of instances, which we solve recursively in time
T_{3}(k^{\cal L})+T_{3}(k^{\cal R})\leq 2T_{3}(3k/4)\leq 2\cdot n^{\mathcal{O}((3k/4)^{2/3})}
. There are two possibilities. (i) At least one of the recursive calls returns that the corresponding sub-instance is a
No
-instance, in which case we proceed to the next guess. (ii) Otherwise, if both of the recursive calls return that
\mathcal{I}^{{\cal L}}
and
\mathcal{I}^{{\cal R}}
are
Yes
-instances along with the respective drawings
\sigma^{\cal L},\sigma^{\cal R}
, then we proceed to the next step.

5.7
Step 5: Conquer Middle.

For each
i\in[3]
, let
M_{i}\coloneqq V_{i}\cap V^{\cal M}\cap S
, where
S={\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf MiddleEdges})\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf CrossingEdges})\cup{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}
as defined in step G. Note that
M_{i}
is a subset of the set
S_{i}
defined in Step H, for which we guess the permutation
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
.

Note that, the relative order among the vertices of
M_{i}
for each
i\in[3]
is already determined by
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
. In particular, note that that
M_{1}=V^{\cal M}\cap V_{1}
, and
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{1}
completely determines the ordering of
M_{1}
. Now we are left with determining the order of vertices in
V^{\cal M}\cap V_{2}
and
V^{\cal M}\cap V_{3}
that respects the partial order
\pi^{\prime}
. To this end, we define an instance of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
, as follows.

Defining a two-layer instance.

Note that the base graph for the instance of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
will be defined on the vertex subsets
V^{\cal M}\cap V_{2}
and
V^{\cal M}\cap V_{3}
, respectively. Later, we will rename them to first and second layer, respectively.

\blacktriangleright
Base graph.

Let
G^{\prime{\cal M}}=G[V^{\cal M}\cap(V_{2}\cap V_{3})]
, with its edge-set partitioned into regular and multi-edges, denoted by
{\sf RegularEdges}^{\cal M}
and
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal M}
, respectively.

\blacktriangleright
Extended boundary.

Let
{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal M}\coloneqq{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},2)\cup{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},2)
, and
{\sf RightExtBnd}^{\cal M}\coloneqq{\sf GapRight}\cup{\sf endpt}({\sf rsep}_{12},2)\cup{\sf endpt}({\sf rsep}_{23},2)
. Then,
{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal M}\coloneqq{\sf LeftExtBnd}^{\cal M}\cup{\sf RightExtBnd}^{\cal M}
.

\blacktriangleright
Boundary edges.

Let
{\sf BoundaryEdges}^{{\cal M}}\coloneqq\left\{{\sf leftbound}^{\cal M},{\sf rightbound}^{\cal M}\right\},
where
{\sf leftbound}^{\cal M}\coloneqq{\sf lsep}_{23},{\sf rightbound}^{\cal L}\coloneqq{\sf rsep}_{23}
.

\blacktriangleright
Multi-edges.

For each (multi-)edge
uv\in{\sf CrossingEdges}\cap E_{23}
with at least one endpoint in
V^{{\cal M}}
, we add a multi-edge to the graph
G^{{\cal M}}
, based on the following cases.

•

vu\in E_{23}
, where
u\in V_{3}
has label
{\cal L}
and
v\in V_{2}
has label
{\cal M}
or
{\cal R}
.

Note that the vertex
v
may belong to
{\sf GapLeft}\subseteq V^{{\cal L}}
, and
u
cannot be equal to
{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3)
.

Operation.
First, if
v\in{\sf GapLeft}\cup\left\{{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},2)\right\}
, then we also delete the edge
vu
from
E(G^{{\cal L}})
. We add a multi-edge
({\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},2),u)
to
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal M}
.

•

uv\in E_{23}
, where
u\in V_{2}
has label
{\cal L}
and
v\in V_{3}
has label
{\cal M}
or
{\cal R}
.

Note that, since
uv\in{\sf CrossingEdges}
, the vertex
u
cannot belong to
{\sf GapLeft}
, and hence cannot have a label
{\cal L}
– this is ensured while guessing the labels.

Operation.
We add a new multi-edge
(u,{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},3))
to
{\sf WeightedEdges}^{\cal M}
.

For each edge considered in each of the preceding cases, if the original edge
uv\in{\sf WeightedEdges}
, then the newly added edge gets multiplicity equal to
\mu(uv)
; otherwise, it gets multiplicity equal to
1
.

Let
G^{\cal M}
denote the resulting graph after adding the edges in this manner.

\blacktriangleright
Vertex set and partial Order.

Let
W_{1}\coloneqq V^{\cal M}\cap V_{2}
and
W_{2}\coloneqq V^{\cal M}\cap V_{3}
, and let
\pi^{\cal M}_{1}=\pi^{\prime}_{2}
projected to the vertices of
W_{1}
, and
\pi^{\cal M}_{2}=\pi^{\prime}_{3}
projected to the vertices of
W_{2}
. Let
\pi^{\cal M}=(\pi^{\cal M}_{1},\pi^{\cal M}_{2})
. Note that,
{\sf GapLeft}
(resp.
{\sf GapRight}
) must contain all the vertices of
V^{conn}_{2}
that are between
{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{23},2)
and
{\sf endpt}({\sf lsep}_{12},2)
(resp.
{\sf endpt}({\sf rsep}_{12},2)
and
{\sf endpt}({\sf rsep}_{23},2)
). Thus, if
(\pi^{\cal M})^{\lambda+2}_{1}
, which is
\pi^{\lambda+2}_{2}
projected to
V_{2}
if violates this condition, then we terminate the guess.

\blacktriangleright
Final instance.

Let
\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}=(G^{\cal M},W^{\cal M}_{1},V^{\cal M}_{2},\pi^{\cal M},k^{\cal M})
.

Finally, we check whether each vertex of
V(G^{\cal M})
is reachable from some vertex of
{\sf ExtBnd}^{\cal M}\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf BoundaryEdges}^{\cal M})
in the constructed graph
G^{\cal M}
. If not, we terminate the guess. Otherwise, it can be verified that the constructed instance satisfies all the properties of an elaborate extended instance of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
from
Definition

14
, which we state in the following claim.

Claim 5.8
.

\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
is a valid elaborate extended instance of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
.

Given this claim, we call the recursive algorithm of
Theorem

6
(without kernelizing) to solve it in time
T_{2}(n,k)\leq n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
. Either, the algorithm returns that
\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
is a
No
-instance, or it returns a drawing
\sigma^{\cal M}
. In the former case, we terminate the guess. Otherwise, we proceed to the following final step.

5.8
Step 6: Obtaining the Final Drawing.

If, for any of the non-terminated guesses, all three recursive calls reporting that the respective sub-instances are
Yes
-instances, along with the corresponding orderings, then we can combine these orderings to obtain orderings of
V_{1},V_{2}
that has at most
k
crossings, and respect the given partial order
\pi
.

Specifically, suppose
\sigma^{\cal L},\sigma^{\cal M},\sigma^{\cal R}
be the drawings of
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
returned by the corresponding recursive calls. Then, we obtain the final drawing of
\mathcal{I}
by defining
\varsigma_{i}\coloneqq\sigma_{i}^{\cal L}\circ\sigma_{i}^{\cal M}\circ\sigma_{i}^{\cal R}
for
i\in[3]
. We return
\varsigma=(\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varsigma_{3})
as our final drawing. In the following lemma, we show that the combined drawing is a valid drawing of
\mathcal{I}
with
k
crossings.

Lemma 19
.

Let
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R},\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
be the three sub-instances defined in Steps 3 and 5 (with respective parameters
k^{\cal L},k^{\cal R},k^{\cal M}
) corresponding to some guess made in Step 2. Further, let
\sigma^{\cal L},\sigma^{\cal R},\sigma^{\cal M}
be valid drawings of the
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R},\sigma^{\cal M}
. Then, if we obtain a combined drawing
\sigma^{\prime}
as in Step 6, then
\sigma^{\prime}
is a valid drawing of
\mathcal{I}
.

Proof.

Recall that, in step H we guess the permutations
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
of
S_{i}
, and we use it in step I to determine the number of crossings
k_{\sf current}
among the edges of
{\sf CrossingEdges}\cup{\sf MiddleEdges}\cup{\sf Sep}
. Then, we define
k^{\prime}=k-k_{\sf current}
, and divide it into
k^{\cal L}+k^{\cal M}+k^{\cal R}
, which are used as parameters for the respective sub-instances.

Next, we claim that all the
k_{\sf current}
crossings among the edges are not counted in any of the sub-instances. To this end, first observe that the edges of
{\sf MiddleEdges}
are not involved in any of the subproblems, since the instance
\mathcal{I}^{\cal M}
is only defined over layers 2 and 3. The proof is analogous to that of
Lemma

7
, except that we need to be careful about the following case. Consider
e_{1},e_{2}\in{\sf CrossingEdges}
where
e_{1}\in{\sf cross}({\sf lsep}_{23})
,
e_{2}\in{\sf cross}({\sf rsep}_{23})
and suppose
e_{1},e_{2}
cross according to
\pi^{\lambda+1}_{i}
. Then, note that the crossing between
e_{1},e_{2}
is a special crossing as defined in Step H, which is not counted in
k_{\sf current}
. Therefore, it implies that any crossing between
e_{1},e_{2}\in{\sf CrossingEdges}
, that contributes towards
k_{\sf current}
must satisfy that
e_{1},e_{2}
cross the same edge of
{\sf Sep}
(corresponding to case (b) in
Lemma

7
). Then the rest of the proof follows.

Next, we note that in
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L}
, the left boundary edges (
{\sf leftbound}_{12},{\sf leftbound}_{23}
) and left extended boundary (
{\sf LeftExtBnd}
) are derived from the current instance
\mathcal{I}
, and thus are placed at the appropriate locations. An analogous property holds about
\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
. Next, we note that the orderings
\pi^{\cal L},\pi^{\cal M},\pi^{\cal R}
is obtained by the projecting
\pi^{\prime}
(which is an extension of
\pi
) to the respective vertex subsets. Since the respective drawings are compatible with these orders, it follows that the combined drawing is compatible with
\pi^{\prime}
, and hence with
\pi
. Furthermore, since
\pi^{\prime}
also contains the orderings
\pi^{\lambda+1}
, the respective drawings also satisfy the ordering on the vertices of
{\sf GapLeft}\cup{\sf GapRight}\cup{\sf ExtBnd}\cup{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},2)
.

Finally, since
\sigma^{\cal L},\sigma^{\cal M},\sigma^{\cal R}
are valid drawings for
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal M},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
, when we obtain the combined drawing
\sigma
by gluing the three drawings, it is straightforward to verify that
{\sf GapLeft}
and
{\sf GapRight}
are placed consecutively at appropriate locations between the vertices of
{\sf Endpoints}({\sf Sep},2)
, and all of the conditions of
Definition

15
are satisfied. This shows that
\sigma^{\prime}
is a valid drawing for
\mathcal{I}
.

∎

Otherwise, for each guess, if either it is terminated, or at least one sub-instance is a
No
-instance, then we return that the current extended instance is a
No
-instance.

Proof of Correctness

Lemma 20

\mathcal{I}

Proof.

In the following claim, we show that the rest of the labeling process correctly labels all the vertices.

Claim 5.9
.

Let
\sigma
be the drawing after appropriate redfinitions as described above, and consider the guess as described above. Then, after the vertex labeling process of step H corresponding to this particular guess, we have that

Proof

.

Let us consider a vertex

,

. We consider different cases based on

and a path
P(uv)
. Let
P(uv)=\langle u=w_{0},w_{1},w_{2},\ldots,w_{\ell}=v\rangle
. Let
w_{i+1}
be the first vertex (if any) along
P(uv)
, such
w_{0},w_{1},\ldots,w_{i}\in V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})
, and
w_{i+1}\not\in V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})
. Otherwise, let
w_{i}=w_{\ell}=v
. We consider these two cases separately.

(i) If
w_{i}=v
, then since

, it cannot be the case that

. Thus, it implies that
w_{i}\in{\sf LeftExtBnd}\cup{\sf Endpoints}(\left\{{\sf leftbound}_{12},{\sf leftbound}_{23}\right\})\cup{\sf CrossLeft}
.

(ii) If
w_{i}\neq v
, then we know that
w_{i+1}
, the vertex immediately after
w_{i}
in

satisfies that
w_{i+1}\notin V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})
. Then, it follows that either the edge
(w_{i},w_{i+1})
crosses some edge of
{\sf Sep}
–implying that
w_{i}\in{\sf CrossLeft}
, or that
w_{i+1}\in V_{3}
, and
w_{i}\in{\sf GapLeft}
.

In any case, for any vertex
u\in V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})\setminus{\sf Origin}^{\cal L}
, and any path

to a vertex

with label

, such that

, and further the entire subpath

does not contain any vertex in

. This also implies that, all the vertices of

are reachable from some vertex

in the graph
G-(O^{{\cal M}}\cup O^{{\cal R}})
. Thus, all the unlabeled vertices in

get labeled

in the label propagation phase. This shows that

. Similar proofs also show that

, and
V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal R})\subseteq V^{\cal R}
. Now, consider a vertex of
u^{\prime}\in(V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})\cup V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal R}))\setminus{\sf Origin}^{\cal L}
. However, such a vertex
u^{\prime}
belongs to
O^{\cal M}\cup O^{\cal R}
, and hence is not part of the graph
G-(O^{\cal M}\cup O^{\cal R})
. Thus, it cannot receive label
{\cal L}
, which shows that

. By combining both containments, we obtain that
V^{\cal L}=V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})
. Similar proofs also show that
V^{\cal M}=V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal L})
, and
V^{\cal R}=V(\sigma,{\sf Sep},{\cal R})
, and are therefore omitted.
∎

After accounting for the

crossings among the endpoints of

in

, the rest of the

Further, since we label all the vertices correctly, it follows that all the vertices of

respectively. Then, we observe that vertices of

(resp.

(resp.

) in the graph
G^{\cal L}
(resp.
G^{\cal R}
), which shows that these guesses are not terminated by the connectivity check done in Step 3 after creating the instances
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
, respectively. This shows that these guesses are not terminated by the connectivity check done in Step 3 after creating the instances
\mathcal{I}^{\cal L},\mathcal{I}^{\cal R}
, respectively. Then,
5.7
imply that

restricted to the respective vertex sets

-instances for the parameters

, respectively. Further, by using an argument similar to the one from

-instances for the respective parameters. Then, by induction (since

of the respective instances, implying that we proceed to Step 4 and create

.

Again, note that all the vertices of

. Again, we observe that vertices of
{\sf CrossMid}
are directly connected to one of the vertices of

-

Reverse direction.

∎

In the following lemma, we argue that the algorithm runs in subexponential time.

Lemma 21
.

For any input extended instance
\mathcal{I}
of
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
with parameter
k
, the algorithm runs in time
T_{3}(k)\leq n^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3})}
.

Proof.

The base case corresponds to when
k\leq c\sqrt{k^{\star}}
, or when
\min\left\{|E_{12}|,|E_{23}|\right\}\leq c\sqrt{k^{\star}}
, for some constant
c
. Then, the running time of the algorithm is
n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k^{\star}})}
via
Lemma

10
.

Otherwise, consider the recursive case, and suppose the claim is true for all
k^{\prime}\leq k-1
, where
k>c\sqrt{k^{\star}}
is the parameter of the given instance
\mathcal{I}
.
Lemma

6
implies that the total number of guesses is bounded by
n^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3})}
, and corresponding to each guess that is not terminated, we make two recursive calls to the algorithm for
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
with parameters at most
3k/4
each. If for one such guess, both recursive calls return a drawing, then we also solve an instance of
2
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
using
Theorem

6
(without the kernelization step), where the parameter is at most
k
. Thus, it takes time
T_{2}(n,k)=n^{\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{k})}
time.
Thus,
T_{3}(n,k)
satisfies the recurrence given in
Equation

2
, and then the claim follows by induction.
∎

By combining
6
,
Lemma

20
,
Lemma

21
, and the fact that due to linear-time kernelization algorithm of
Theorem

4
, we have
n=(k^{\star})^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
, we obtain the following theorem (where we rename
k^{\star}
as
k
).

Theorem 7
.

On an
n
vertex graph,
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
can be solved in time
2^{\mathcal{O}(k^{2/3}\log k)}+n\cdot k^{\mathcal{O}(1)}
, where
k
denotes the number of crossings.

6
Kernelization for
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization

The whole section is the proof of
Theorem

4
, which we restate here.

See
4

In
Section

6.1
, we give the kernelization algorithm and prove its correctness. In
Section

6.2
, we show that using some additional steps, we can implement the algorithm to run in time which is linear in
n
.

6.1
Construction of the kernel

Let
(G,V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},k)
where
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
is a
3
-partition of
V(G)
be an instance of
3
-
Layer Crossing Minimization
.
We give a polynomial time algorithm that either correctly concludes that
(G,V_{1},V_{2},V_{3},k)
is a no-instance, or constructs an equivalent instance
(G^{\prime},V^{\prime}_{1},V^{\prime}_{2},V^{\prime}_{3},k)
, with
\mathcal{O}(k^{8})
vertices and edges.

By
Proposition

1
, one can check in time
\mathcal{O}(n)
whether
G
admits a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
without crossings. If this is the case, we immediately return a trivial yes-instance and stop. We also stop and output a trivial no-instance if
k=0
and
G
does not admit such a drawing. From now on, we assume that
G
has no drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
without crossings and that
k\geq 1
.

We apply a sequence of reduction rules. Almost each of the rules operates as follows: Suppose
G
contains a sufficiently large subgraph
H
that can be (a) separated from
G
by deleting a small (at most 6) number of vertices and (b) drawn on two or three layers without crossing. Then, we derive an equivalent but smaller instance of the problem by removing some “irrelevant” vertices of
H
from
G
. Occasionally, we may need to introduce new edges, but the number of vertices in the new equivalent instance always decreases.
None of the rules changes the parameter
k
.
The rules and the proof of their correctness (or, using the established expression in the area, their safety) depend on the number of vertices required to separate
H
from
G
. The rules are exhaustively applied whenever possible. If, by a rule, we delete a vertex or a set of vertices, we assume that the sets
V_{1},V_{2},V_{3}
are modified accordingly by the deletion of vertices.

First, we address connected components that can be drawn without crossings. Trivially, such components are irrelevant because they can be drawn independently without creating any crossings.

Reduction Rule 6.1
.

If
G
has a connected component
H
that admits a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1}\cap V(H),V_{2}\cap V(H),V_{3}\cap V(H))
without crossings, then set
G:=G-V(H)
.

For a vertex
v\in V(G)
and
i\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
P_{i}(v)
be the set of pendent vertices in
V_{i}
adjacent to
v
.
The following reduction rule
reduces the number of pendent neighbors of the vertices from
V_{2}
.

Reduction Rule 6.2
.

If there is
u\in V_{2}
such that
|P_{i}(u)|>k+1
for some
i\in\{1,3\}
then select (arbitrarily) vertex
v\in P_{i}(u)
and
set
G:=G-v
.

Lemma 22
.

6.2
is safe.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that the rule is applied for
u\in V_{2}
and
v\in P_{1}(u)
, and let
G^{\prime}=G-v
and
V_{1}^{\prime}=V_{1}\setminus\{v\}
. Trivially, if
G
admits a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing then
G^{\prime}
has a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing. Suppose that
G^{\prime}
admits a
3
-layer drawing

\sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
respecting
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing. Because
|P_{1}(u)|>k+1
,
u
is adjacent to at least
k+1
pendent vertices in
V_{1}^{\prime}
. Since the number of crossings is upper bounded by
k
, there is
w\in P_{1}(u)\setminus\{v\}
such that
uw
does not cross any other edge. We construct the order
\sigma_{1}
of
V_{1}
from
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
by inserting
v
immediately after
w
. Then
uv
does not cross any edge of
G
. Thus,
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
is
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing. This concludes the proof.
∎

We apply a similar rule to reduce the number of vertices in
V_{2}
. Let
u\in V_{1}
and
v\in V_{3}
. We denote by
Q(u,v)
the set of vertices of
V_{2}
of degree two, that is,
u
and
v
are the only neighbors of any
w\in Q(u,v)
.

Reduction Rule 6.3
.

If there are
u\in V_{1}
and
v\in V_{3}
such that
|Q(u,v)|>k+1
then select (arbitrarily) vertex

w\in Q(u,v)
and
set
G:=G-w
.

Lemma 23
.

6.2
is safe.

Proof.

Suppose that the rule is applied for
u\in V_{1}
,
v\in V_{3}
, and
w\in Q(u,v)
, and let
G^{\prime}=G-w
and
V_{2}^{\prime}=V_{2}\setminus\{w\}
. As in the poof of
Lemma

22
, it is sufficient to show that if
G^{\prime}
has a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2}^{\prime},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing then
G
has a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing. Assume that
\sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{3})
is such a drawing of
G^{\prime}
. Because
|Q(u,v)|>k+1
, there is
x\in Q(u,v)\setminus\{w\}
such that edges
ux
and
vx
do not cross any edge in the drawing. We construct the order
\sigma_{2}
of
V_{2}
from
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
by inserting
w
immediately after
w
. Then, neither
uw
nor
vw
cross an edge of
G
. We obtain that
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
is
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing. This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

Let
u\in V_{1}\cup V_{3}
be a cut vertex of
G
.
We say that a connected component
H
of
G-u
is
nice
if (i)
V(H)\subseteq V_{2}\cup V_{3}
if
u\in V_{1}
and
V(H)\subseteq V_{1}\cup V_{2}
if
u\in V_{3}
, and (ii)
H
admits a
2
-layer drawing without crossings respecting either
(V_{2}\cap V(H),V_{3}\cap V(H))
if
u\in V_{1}
or
(V_{1}\cap V(H),V_{2}\cap V(H))
if
u\in V_{3}
. The next rule is used to reduce the number of nice components.

Reduction Rule 6.4
.

If there is
u\in V_{1}\cup V_{3}
such that the number of nice connected components of
G-u
is at least
2k+2
, then select a nice component
H
and set
G:=G-V(H)
; we select a nice component of size one if such a component
H
exists and choose arbitrary
H
, otherwise.

Lemma 24
.

6.4
is safe.

Proof.

By symmetry, let us assume that the rule is applied for
u\in V_{1}
and
V(H)\subseteq V_{2}\cup V_{3}
for the deleted nice component
H
. Let
G^{\prime}=G-V(H)
,
V_{2}^{\prime}=V_{2}\setminus V(H)
, and
V_{3}^{\prime}=V_{3}\setminus V(H)
.
Again, it is sufficient to show that if
G^{\prime}
has a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2}^{\prime},V_{3}^{\prime})
with at most
k
crossing then
G
has a
3
-layer drawing respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossing. Assume that
\sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{3}^{\prime})
is a
3
-layer drawing of
G^{\prime}
with at most
k
crossings. Because the number of nice connected components of
G-u
is at least
2k+2
, there is a nice component
C
distinct from
H
such that neither edges of
C
nor edges
uv
for
v\in V(C)
cross other edges. Since we cannot apply
6.1
,
C
cannot be a connected component of
G
. Thus, there is a vertex of
C
adjacent to
u
.
Denote by
x
the last, with respect to
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
, vertex of
V_{2}\cap V(C)
adjacent to
u
.

Suppose first that
H
is trivial, that is, it has a single vertex. Then we construct a
3
-layer drawing
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{3})
of
G
respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
by modifying
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
the same way as in the proof of
Lemma

22
. Namely, we insert the unique vertex of
H
immediately after
x
in
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
. It does not create a new crossing because
ux
does not cross any edge in
\sigma^{\prime}
.

From now on, we assume that
H
has at least two vertices. By the choice of
H
, there is no nice component of size one and, therefore,
C
also has at least two vertices and has vertices from both
V_{2}
and
V_{3}
.
Let
y
be the last vertex of
V_{2}\cap V(C)
and
z
be the last vertex of
V_{3}\cap V(C)
with respect to
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{3}^{\prime}
, respectively. It may happen that
x=y
. Suppose that there is
v\in V_{2}^{\prime}\setminus V(C)
such that
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(x)<\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(v)<\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(y)
. Because
C
is connected, we have that
v
has no neighbors in
V_{3}
and is adjacent only to some vertices

w\in V_{1}
with
\sigma_{1}(u)\leq\sigma_{2}(w)
. This observation allows us to modify
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
as follows. Let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
be all the vertices of
V_{2}\setminus V(C)
such that
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(x)<\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(v_{i})<\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(y)
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,\ell\}
(the set of these vertices may be empty) and assume that

\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(v_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{2}^{\prime}(v_{\ell})
. We construct
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
from
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
by placing
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
following their order in
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
after
y
in the order of
V_{2}^{\prime}
. Notice that this modification does not create any new crossing. Furthermore, for
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime},\sigma_{3}^{\prime})
, we have that either
u
,
y
, and
z
are the last vertices in

\sigma_{1}
,
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\sigma_{3}^{\prime}
, respectively, or

u
separates the vertices that are after
u
,
y
, and
z
with respect to
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
from the vertices that are before them. More formally,
it holds that (a) there is no edge
ab
with
a\in V_{2}^{\prime}
and
b\in V_{3}^{\prime}
such that either
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(a)\leq\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(y)
and
\sigma_{3}^{\prime}(b)>\sigma_{3}^{\prime}(z)
or
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(a)>\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(y)
and
\sigma_{3}^{\prime}(b)\leq\sigma_{3}^{\prime}(z)
, and (b) there is no edge
ab
with
a\in V_{1}
and
b\in V_{2}^{\prime}
such that either

\sigma_{1}(a)<\sigma_{a}(u)
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(b)>\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(y)
or
\sigma_{1}(a)>\sigma_{1}(u)
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(b)\leq\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}(y)
. This allows us to construct a
3
-layer drawing
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
of
G
respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
without increasing the number of crossings.

Because
H
admits a
2
-layer drawing without crossings respecting
(V_{2}\cap V(H),V_{3}\cap V(H))
, there are the corresponding orders
\hat{\sigma}_{2}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{3}
of
V_{2}\cap V(H)
and
V_{3}\cap V(H)
, respectively. We construct
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
by modifying
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{3}^{\prime}
. To construct
\sigma_{2}
, we insert the vertices of
V_{2}\cap V(H)
in
\sigma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
immediately after
y
following
\hat{\sigma}_{2}
. Similarly,
\sigma_{3}
is constructed from
\sigma_{3}^{\prime}
by inserting the vertices of
V_{3}\cap V(H)
immediately after
z
following
\hat{\sigma}_{3}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

By the following five rules, we detect pieces of the graph with only particular drawings and reduce their size. We start with pieces that can be separated by four vertices from the remaining graph.

Figure 25:
Applying
6.5
; the vertices of
A
are black bullets and the vertices of
B\setminus A
are white; the edges of
H
are solid lines and the edges outside
H
are drawn by dashed lines; the edges of
M
are highlighted by thick lines.

Informally,
6.5
does the following. Assume that
H
is a subgraph of
G
that has a
2
-layer drawing in layers

V_{1}
and
V_{2}
without crossings. Moreover, assume that in such a drawing, the endpoints of the leftmost edge
u_{1}v_{1}
and the rightmost edge
u_{2}v_{2}
of
H
separate it from the remaining part of
G
. Then, if
H
contains a matching
M
of size
4k+3
, at least one of the endpoints of the central edge in
M
could be safely removed. (We also must add one or two edges to maintain connectivity.)

Reduction Rule 6.5

(4-Separation A)
.

Suppose that there is a separation
(A,B)
of
G
with
A\cap B=\{u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}\}
such that (see
Figure

26
)

(i)

H=G[A]
is a connected graph with
A\subseteq V_{1}\cup V_{2}
,
u_{1},u_{2}\in V_{1}
, and
v_{1},v_{2}\in V_{2}
,

(ii)

u_{1}v_{1},u_{2}v_{2}\in E(G)
,

(iii)

H
has a
2
-layer drawing
\sigma_{H}=(\sigma_{1}^{H},\sigma_{2}^{H})
without crossings respecting
(V_{1}\cap V(H),V_{2}\cap V(H))
, where
\sigma_{i}^{H}
is an order of
V_{i}\cap V(H)
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, such that
(a) for every
w\in V_{1}
,
\sigma_{1}^{H}(u_{1})\leq\sigma_{1}^{H}(w)\leq\sigma_{1}^{H}(u_{2})
and (b) for every
w\in V_{2}
,
\sigma_{2}^{H}(v_{1})\leq\sigma_{2}^{H}(w)\leq\sigma_{2}^{H}(v_{2})
,

(iv)

H
has a matching
M
of size
4k+3
with
u_{1}v_{1},u_{2}v_{2}\in M
.

Then let
M=\{x_{1}y_{1},\ldots,x_{4k+3}y_{4k+3}\}
, where
x_{1}=u_{1}
,
y_{1}=v_{1}
,
x_{4k+3}=u_{2}
,
y_{4k+3}=v_{2}
,
\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{4k+3})
, and

\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{4k+3})
, and do the following:

(a)

find the unique
z_{1}\in\{x_{2k+1},y_{2k+1}\}
that has a neighbor either after
x_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{1}^{H}
or after
y_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{2}^{H}
, respectively, and find
the unique
z_{2}\in\{x_{2k+3},y_{2k+3}\}
that has a neighbor either before
x_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{1}^{H}
or before
y_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{2}^{H}
, respectively,

(b)

delete every vertex
w\in V_{1}\cap V(H)
with
\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{2k+1})<\sigma_{1}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{2k+3})
,

(c)

delete every vertex
w\in V_{2}\cap V(H)
with
\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{2k+1})<\sigma_{2}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{2k+3})
,

(d)

if either
z_{1}\in V_{1}
and
z_{2}\in V_{2}
or
z_{1}\in V_{2}
and
z_{2}\in V_{1}
then make
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
adjacent to maintain connectivity,

(e)

if
z_{1}\in V_{1}
and
z_{2}\in V_{1}
then restore
y_{2i+2}
and make it adjacent to
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
,

(f)

if
z_{1}\in V_{2}
and
z_{2}\in V_{2}
then restore
x_{2i+2}
and make it adjacent to
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
.

We note that we use a complicated procedure of adding edges to ensure connectivity for simplifying the running time analysis in the next subsection. In particular, we guarantee that the rule does not create new pendent vertices and ensure that the graph
H^{\prime}
obtained from
H
does not contain a matching of the same size as
M
if
M
is a maximum size matching satisfying the conditions of the rule.

Lemma 25
.

6.5
is safe.

Proof.

First, we observe that because
H
has a
2
-layer drawing
\sigma_{H}=(\sigma_{1}^{H},\sigma_{2}^{H})
without crossings respecting
(V_{1}\cap V(H),V_{2}\cap V(H))
such that (iii)(a) and (iii)(b) are fulfilled, we can order the endpoints of the edges of the matching
M
to satisfy the conditions that
\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{4k+3})
, and

\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{4k+3})
. Thus, the rule can be executed if there is a separation
(A,B)
satisfying (i)–(iv). Note also that because at least one of the vertices
x_{2k+2}
and
y_{2k+2}
is deleted by the rule, it reduces the size of the graph.

Suppose that the rule is applied for a separation
(A,B)
and denote by
G^{\prime}
the graph obtained by applying the rule. Let also
D\subseteq V_{1}\cup V_{2}
be the set of deleted vertices,
V_{1}^{\prime}=V_{1}\setminus D
,
V_{2}^{\prime}=V_{2}\setminus D
.

Assume that
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
is a
3
-layer drawing of
G
respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossings. Notice that because the number of crossings is at most
k
, there are
i\in\{1,\ldots,2k+1\}
and
j\in\{2k+3,\ldots,4k+3\}
such that the edges
x_{i}y_{i}
and
x_{j}y_{j}
do not cross any edge. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\sigma_{1}(x_{i})<\sigma_{1}(x_{j})
and
\sigma_{2}(y_{i})<\sigma_{2}(y_{j})
. Otherwise, we just reverse the orders in
\sigma
.
We set

U_{1}=\{w\in V_{1}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma_{1}(x_{i})<\sigma_{1}(w)<\sigma_{1}(x_{j})\}

and

U_{2}=\{w\in V_{2}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma_{2}(y_{i})<\sigma_{2}(w)<\sigma_{2}(y_{j})\},

and define

W_{1}=\{w\in V_{1}\setminus V(H)\colon\sigma_{1}(x_{i})<\sigma_{1}(w)<\sigma_{1}(x_{j})\}

and

W_{2}=\{w\in V_{2}\setminus V(H)\colon\sigma_{2}(y_{i})<\sigma_{2}(w)<\sigma_{2}(y_{j})\}.

Observe that because
H
is connected and
x_{i}y_{i}
and
x_{j}y_{j}
do not cross any edge, we have that

U_{1}=\{w\in V_{1}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{i})<\sigma_{1}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{j})\}

and

U_{2}=\{w\in V_{2}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{i})<\sigma_{2}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{j})\}.

Furthermore, if
xy\in E(G)\setminus E(H)
with
x\in V_{1}
and
y\in V_{2}
such that
x\in W_{1}
or
y\in W_{2}
, then
x\in W_{1}
,
y\in W_{2}
, and
xy
crosses at least one edge of
H
in the drawing of
G
.

We construct a
3
-layer drawing
\sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{2},\sigma_{3})
of
G^{\prime}
respecting
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime},V_{3})
with no more crossing than
\sigma
as follows.
We obtain
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
from
\sigma_{1}
by reordering the vertices of
U_{1}\cap V(G^{\prime})
and
W_{1}
.
First, we place the vertices of
U_{1}\cap V(G^{\prime})
between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
following their order in
\sigma^{H}_{1}
and then place the vertices of
W_{1}
between
x_{2k+1}
and
x_{2k+3}
following their order in
\sigma_{1}
.
Similarly,

\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
is obtained from
\sigma_{2}
by reordering the vertices of
U_{2}\cap V(G^{\prime})
and
W_{2}
:
we place the vertices of
U_{2}\cap V(G^{\prime})
between
y_{i}
and
y_{j}
following their order in
\sigma^{H}_{2}
and then place the vertices of
W_{2}
between

y_{2k+1}
and
y_{2k+3}
following their order in
\sigma_{2}
. Observe that an edge
xy\in E(H)\cap E(G^{\prime})
with
\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{i})\leq\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x)\leq\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{j})
and

\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{i})\leq\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y)\leq\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(y_{j})
does not cross any edge of
G^{\prime}
. Further,
the edges constructed by the rules, that is, either
x_{2k+1}y_{2k+3}
, or
y_{2k+1}x_{2k+3}
, or
x_{2k+1}y_{2k+2}
with
y_{2k+2}x_{2k+3}
, or
y_{2k+1}x_{2k+2}
with
x_{2k+2}y_{2k+3}
,
cross only the edges
xy\in E(G^{\prime})
with
x\in W_{1}
and
y\in W_{2}
and
xy
crosses exactly one edge constructed by the rule. This implies that in

\sigma^{\prime}
the number of crossings does not exceed the number of crossings in
\sigma
.

For the opposite direction, assume that there is a
3
-layer drawing
\sigma^{\prime}=(\sigma^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{2},\sigma_{3})
of
G^{\prime}
respecting
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime},V_{3})
with at most
k
crossings. We are using almost the same arguments as for the forward implication to show that there is a
3
-layer drawing
\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
of
G
respecting
(V_{1},V_{2},V_{3})
such that the number of crossings in
\sigma
is at most the number of crossings in
\sigma^{\prime}
.

Because the number of crossings is at most
k
, there are
i\in\{1,\ldots,2k+1\}
and
j\in\{2k+3,\ldots,4k+3\}
such that the edges
x_{i}y_{i}
and
x_{j}y_{j}
do not cross any edge with respect to
\sigma^{\prime}
. We can assume that
\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{i})<\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{j})
and
\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{i})<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{j})
.
We define vertex sets

U_{1}=\{w\in V_{1}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{i})<\sigma_{1}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{1}^{H}(x_{j})\},

U_{2}=\{w\in V_{2}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{i})<\sigma_{2}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{2}^{H}(y_{j})\}.

We also introduce

U_{1}^{\prime}=\{w\in V_{1}^{\prime}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{i})<\sigma_{1}(w)<\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{j})\}

and

U_{2}^{\prime}=\{w\in V_{2}^{\prime}\cap V(H)\colon\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{i})<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(w)<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{j})\}.

We further define

W_{1}^{\prime}=\{w\in V_{1}\setminus V(H)\colon\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{i})<\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(w)<\sigma^{\prime}_{1}(x_{j})\},

and

W_{2}^{\prime}=\{w\in V_{2}\setminus V(H)\colon\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{i})<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(w)<\sigma^{\prime}_{2}(y_{j})\}.

We remark that graph
H^{\prime}
obtained from
H
by deleting the vertices of
D
and
adding one or two edges
is connected.
Then, since
x_{i}y_{i}
and
x_{j}y_{j}
do not cross any edge, we have that

U_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq U_{1}
and
U_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq U_{2}
.
Moreover, if
xy\in E(G^{\prime})\setminus E(H^{\prime})
with
x\in V_{1}
and
y\in V_{2}
such that
x\in W_{1}
or
y\in W_{2}
, then
x\in W_{1}
and
y\in W_{2}
and
xy
crosses at least one edge of
H^{\prime}
in the drawing of
G^{\prime}
.

We construct
\sigma_{1}
from
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
.
First, we replace the vertices of
U_{1}^{\prime}
with the vertices of
U_{1}
and put them between
x_{i}
and
x_{j}
following their order in
\sigma^{H}_{1}
. Then we place the vertices of
W_{1}
immediately after
x_{2k+2}
following their order in
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
.
The order

\sigma_{2}
is constructed from
\sigma^{\prime}_{2}
in a similar way. We replace the vertices of
U_{2}^{\prime}
with the vertices of
U_{2}
and put them between
y_{i}
and
y_{j}
following their order in
\sigma^{H}_{2}
. Then we place the vertices of
W_{2}
immediately before
y_{2k+2}
following their order in
\sigma^{\prime}_{2}
.
We have that any edge
xy\in E(H)
with
\sigma_{1}(x_{i})\leq\sigma_{1}(x)\leq\sigma_{1}(x_{j})
and

\sigma_{2}(y_{i})\leq\sigma_{2}(y)\leq\sigma_{1}(y_{j})
that is distinct from
x_{2k+2}y_{2k+2}

does not cross any edge of
G

and
x_{2k+1}y_{2k+3}

crosses only the edges
xy\in E(G)
with
x\in W_{1}
and
y\in W_{2}
. Therefore, the number of crossings in the constructed
3
-layer drawing
\sigma
does not exceed the number of crossings in
\sigma^{\prime}
. This concludes the proof of the claim.
∎

6.5
is applied for the drawing of
H
in the layers
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.
Of course, by symmetry, the same arguments are applicable when the vertices of
H
are in layers
V_{2}
and
V_{3}
.
Since the statement of
6.5
is already quite long and challenging to read. Thus, instead of putting it in the general form, capturing two symmetric cases (like we did in
6.2
), it is slightly preferable to have it as two reduction rules.
The next
6.6
, up to the symmetry, is exactly the same as
6.5
.
We state the rule (without proof of its safety) for completeness.

Reduction Rule 6.6

(4-Separation B)
.

Suppose that there is a separation
(A,B)
of
G
with a separator
A\cap B=\{u_{1},u_{2},v_{1},v_{2}\}
such that

(i)

H=G[A]
is a connected graph with
A\subseteq V_{2}\cup V_{3}
,
u_{1},u_{2}\in V_{2}
, and
v_{1},v_{2}\in V_{3}
,

(ii)

u_{1}v_{1},u_{2}v_{2}\in E(G)
,

(iii)

H
has a
2
-layer drawing
\sigma_{H}=(\sigma_{2}^{H},\sigma_{3}^{H})
without crossings respecting
(V_{2}\cap V(H),V_{3}\cap V(H))
, where
\sigma_{i}^{H}
is an order of
V_{i}\cap V(H)
for
i\in\{2,3\}
, such that
(a) for every
w\in V_{2}
,
\sigma_{2}^{H}(u_{1})\leq\sigma_{2}^{H}(w)\leq\sigma_{2}^{H}(u_{2})
and (b) for every
w\in V_{3}
,
\sigma_{3}^{H}(v_{1})\leq\sigma_{3}^{H}(w)\leq\sigma_{3}^{H}(v_{2})
,

(iv)

H
has a matching
M
of size
4k+3
with
u_{1}v_{1},u_{2}v_{2}\in M
.

Then let
M=\{x_{1}y_{1},\ldots,x_{4k+3}y_{4k+3}\}
, where
x_{1}=u_{1}
,
y_{1}=v_{1}
,
x_{4k+3}=u_{2}
,
y_{4k+3}=v_{2}
,
\sigma_{2}^{H}(x_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{2}^{H}(x_{4k+3})
, and

\sigma_{3}^{H}(y_{1})<\dots<\sigma_{3}^{H}(y_{4k+3})
, and do the following:

(a)

find the unique
z_{1}\in\{x_{2k+1},y_{2k+1}\}
that has a neighbor either after
x_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{2}^{H}
or after
y_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{3}^{H}
, respectively, and find
the unique
z_{2}\in\{x_{2k+3},y_{2k+3}\}
that has a neighbor either before
x_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{2}^{H}
or before
y_{2k+1}
in
\sigma_{3}^{H}
, respectively,

(b)

delete every vertex
w\in V_{1}\cap V(H)
with
\sigma_{2}^{H}(x_{2k+1})<\sigma_{2}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{2}^{H}(x_{2k+3})
,

(c)

delete every vertex
w\in V_{2}\cap V(H)
with
\sigma_{3}^{H}(y_{2k+1})<\sigma_{3}^{H}(w)<\sigma_{3}^{H}(y_{2k+3})
